## Document 021: 2018年噩梦重演？走势高度相似，纳斯达克反弹之后或还有下一波回调

Source PDF: all report pdf/2018年噩梦重演？走势高度相似，纳斯达克反弹之后或还有下一波回调.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

美股跌⼊极端超卖区间，对冲基⾦以近⼗年来最快速度削减仓位，技术性反弹信号逐渐显
现。但美联储仍处于加息周期，资⾦⼊场意愿不⾜。 2018 年四季度，纳指曾在急跌后反
弹，随后再度下探并创出新低。当前市场同样⾯临加息压⼒与⻛险偏好低迷的双重制约。
摘要：
美股抛售潮已将市场推⾄极端超卖状态，但历史经验显⽰，急跌后的反弹未必意味着市场已经
触底，纳斯达克可能仍⾯临新⼀轮回调。
标普 500 指数已跌⼊极度超卖区间，科技股波动率飙升⾄ 2020 年以来最⾼⽔平，对冲基⾦也在以
近⼗年来最快的速度削减仓位。市场恐慌程度与资⾦撤退规模，均已达到罕⻅⽔平。
⾼盛数据显⽰，其恐慌指标（ Vol Panic Index ）上周末收于 9.8 ，满分为 10 。历史上，这⼀⽔平
通常是强烈的逆向加仓信号。但当前美联储仍处于加息周期，情况有所不同：此前恐慌指数超
过 9 且美联储同步加息时，标普 500 指数未来三个⽉平均仅上涨 0.4% 。
更值得警惕的是，当前市场⾛势与 2018 年纳斯达克的⼀轮调整⾼度相似。当年市场在急跌后曾

出现快速反弹，但随后再度下探并创出新低。如今，随着科技股抛售加剧，类似的历史剧本再
次引发市场关注。
科技股恐慌升⾄极值，反弹条件逐步成熟
当前标普 500 指数的超卖程度已处于极端⽔平，同时正在测试⾃ 2022 年底低点以来形成的⻓期趋
势线。
科技股的压⼒更加突出。纳斯达克波动率指数（ VXN ）已升⾄ 2020 年 4 ⽉以来最⾼⽔平，超过此
前市场剧烈调整时期的峰值。半导体波动率指数（ VXSMH ）则创下历史新⾼，尽管该指数的数
据仅始于 2022 年。上周五，费城半导体指数（ SOX ）单⽇暴跌及随之⽽来的波动率飙升，更被
市场⼈⼠形容为 “ 载⼊史册 ” 。
从估值来看，部分科技股也已出现明显回调。⾼盛的彼得 · 卡拉汉（ Peter Callahan ）指出，纳
斯达克 100 指数远期市盈率已降⾄ 17 倍，半导体板块更低⾄ 12 ⾄ 13 倍，技术⾯超卖信号明确。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

但估值回落尚未带来明显的抄底需求。卡拉汉指出，当前市场⾛势已经反映企业盈利下调预
期，但实际基本⾯仍相对稳健，盈利预测依然在上修。市场价格与盈利趋势之间的背离，说明
本轮抛售在⼀定程度上受到⻛险偏好下降和仓位调整驱动，⽽⾮企业盈利已经全⾯恶化。
对冲基⾦⼤幅去杠杆，抛售压⼒接近释放尾声？
投资者仓位的变化进⼀步凸显了本轮调整的剧烈程度。
⾼盛主经纪商业务数据显⽰，上周美股单只股票做空规模达到 2022 年 6 ⽉以来最⾼单周⽔平，也
是近⼗年来第三⾼； ETF 空头头⼨单周增加 11% ，创五年来最⼤单周增幅之⼀。上述数据仅统计
⾄上周四，尚未计⼊周五的抛售。
对冲基⾦的净杠杆率也已降⾄极低⽔平。基本⾯多空策略（ L/S ）净杠杆率降⾄ 42.3% ，处于过
去⼀年分布区间的第 1 百分位；经⻉塔调整后的净杠杆率⾃ 10 ⽉初以来累计下降逾 22 个百分点，
⾄ 48.2% ，创五年新低。
⾼盛认为，此轮去杠杆可能已经接近尾声，⽽⾮刚刚开始。不过，市场尚未完全摆脱潜在卖
压： CFTC ⾮商业交易者持仓仍接近⾼位，意味着部分拥挤头⼨仍有进⼀步调整的空间。

与此同时， CTA 策略在纳斯达克 100 期货上的仓位已接近过去⼗年的区间底部。过去六周，系统
性资⾦抛售科技股的速度更创下历史纪录。主动型对冲基⾦与量化资⾦同步减仓，也使科技股
持续承受被动卖盘压⼒。
短伽⻢放⼤双向波动， 2018 年⾛势值得警惕

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

6 条评论
即便去杠杆可能接近尾声，市场也未必会就此企稳。当前⼤量短伽⻢（ Short Gamma ）头⼨意
味着，做市商在市场下跌时可能被迫卖出，上涨时则需要买⼊，从⽽放⼤市场的双向波动。在
波动率已经处于极端⽔平的情况下，这种仓位结构可能令⾏情更加剧烈。
2018 年的历史⾛势，正是当前市场需要警惕的⻛险之⼀。当年纳斯达克经历快速下跌后，曾出
现⼀轮强劲反弹，但反弹未能扭转市场趋势，随后指数再次下探并创出新低。⾼盛及市场观察
⼈⼠⽬前正密切关注这⼀历史⾛势与当下⾏情的相似之处。
这意味着，极端超卖和仓位出清虽然为反弹创造了条件，却不⾜以证明市场已经触底。尤其是
在美联储仍处于加息周期、⻛险偏好尚未恢复的背景下，即使纳斯达克短期反弹，也不排除随
后再次回调。

眼下市场真正需要验证的，不是超卖信号能否触发反弹，⽽是反弹能否获得基本⾯和资⾦⾯的
持续⽀撑。如果抄底资⾦迟迟不⼊场， 2018 年急弹后再创新低的⾛势，仍可能重演。

## Document 025: 2026-11-13_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］160円台はやはり「円安すぎ」ではない_b6d02f4b

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-11-13_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］160円台はやはり「円安すぎ」ではない_b6d02f4b.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

160 円台はやはり「円安すぎ」ではない 
～164 円への円安も視野、介入警戒の磁力が一段安を阻むか～

調査部  主任エコノミスト 東深澤 武史 
080-1069-4919  takeshi.higashifukasawa@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ ドル円の安定はかりそめ 
9月下旬以降、1ドル＝150円台後半での小動きが続いたが、これをもって「相場が安定している」と
捉えるのは危うい。チャート上は、上値目途として意識される200日移動平均線が150円台半ばに位置
し、同水準へ向けた円安方向への動きが繰り返された（図表1）。このところの値動きの狭さは、円安
方向を根強く試す動きが抵抗線付近で抑えられた結果とみる方が実態に近そうだ。 
11月に入ってからは、円高になりうる要因が散見されたにもかかわらず、市場ではあまり材料視さ
れていない。米国中間選挙（11月3日）の投開票後には、米国のデータセンター建設投資の持続性に対
する懸念が強まったことで株価が大きく下落した（図表2）。震源地である米国から日本に資金を退避
する動きが生じれば円高要因になるが、ドル円の値動きは限定的だった。また、中東情勢に対する楽
観的な見方から原油安が進展しており、円需給の観点からは先行きの本邦輸入金額の減少を通じて円
安圧力を緩和する要因になるが、この点も現時点では円高につながっていない。 
足元では、米利上げ観測の再燃を背景に再び円安が進んでおり、円安圧力は一段と強まっている。
本稿執筆時点では、心理的節目の200日移動平均線を超え、159円台後半まで上昇している。 
図表1 ドル円レート 図表2 日米株価指数

### Page 2

本稿では、円安圧力の背景にある要因を改めて整理したうえで、それらを考慮したドル円の推計値
を試算する。実勢値との比較を通じて、足元の円安水準が現在の経済環境に照らしてどの程度説明で
きるのか検証し、その結果をもとに先行きのドル円を展望する。 
■ 現時点でのドル円の適正水準は 1 ドル＝163.70 円 
1ドル＝160円の円安を試す動きは、現在の経済環境に裏付けられたものなのだろうか。この点を検
証するために、東深澤（2026）で構築したモデルを用いて、現在の経済環境から説明されるドル円の
水準を確認する（推計式の詳細や構築過程については東深澤（2026）を参照されたい）。このモデル
を用いて足元の経済環境と整合的なドル円を試算すると、推計値は1ドル＝163.70円と実勢値より円安
になる（図表3）。足元の経済環境下では、160円台はやはり「円安すぎ」ではないということだ。 
モデルが円安水準を支持している理由として最も大きいのが、日本の期待インフレ率の上昇だ。2022
年以降の円安を要因分解すると、米金利上昇や実需の円需給環境の悪化に加え、日本の期待インフレ
率の上昇が大きく寄与している（図表4）。物価が上がれば、同じ金額で買える財・サービスが少なく
なり、円の購買力は低下する。先行きも物価上昇が続くとの見方が強まれば（＝期待インフレ率の上
昇）、将来の円の価値が低下することを織り込む過程で、現在の円相場にも円安圧力がかかると考え
られる。 
実際、日本の期待インフレ率上昇と円安との関係は、データからも確認される。図表5をみると、日
米期待インフレ率格差が縮小すると、円安が進むという対応関係がうかがえる。米国の期待インフレ
率はおおむね安定しており1、日米期待インフレ率格差の縮小は、主に日本の期待インフレ率が上昇し
ていることによる。こうした期待インフレ率の高まりは、賃金・物価上昇の定着にともなう構造的な
変化2だ。期待インフレ率が反転して大幅に低下することは短期的に見込みづらいため、1ドル＝160円
程度の円安水準が定着することは自然だと言える。 
加えて、足元では米金利の上昇も円安圧力となっているようだ。11月以降は、市場予想を上回る物

### Page 3

3

価指標や、ウォラー理事など複数のFRB高官発言がタカ派的と受け止められたことを背景に、米金利
は再度上昇に転じつつある。 
■ 今後は、介入警戒の磁力が一段の円安を阻むかが焦点に 
先行き、推計値（1ドル=163.70円）との乖離を解消する形で円安が進む可能性は十分にある。円安進
行のドライバーになりうるのが、投機筋に積み上がった円買いポジションの巻き戻しだ。図表6は、実
勢値と推計値の乖離を、投機筋の円ポジションに重ねたものだ。これをみると、円買いポジションが
拡大する局面では、実勢値が推計値より円高になる場面が多いことがわかる。とりわけ9月中旬にかけ
ては、日銀の追加利上げ観測などを背景に投機筋の円買い超過幅が拡大した。円買いポジション調整
の余地は十分にあり、巻き戻しの動きが顕在化する場合は1ドル＝164円近傍までの円安進行が視野に
入ってくるということだ。 
こうしたシナリオが実現するかどうかは、為替介入への警戒の持続性に左右される。7月末の日米協
調介入も記憶に新しく、今のところ為替市場は介入への警戒感を維持していると考えられる。11月12
日には三村財務官が円安をけん制する発言を行うなど、日本政府が再び介入姿勢を強めている点を踏
まえれば、160円を持続的に上回るような円安進行は回避されるという見方が穏当だ。 
ただし、リスクは円安方向に大きい。特に、協調介入に対する米国側の姿勢が反転するシナリオに
注意しておきたい。7月当時と比べると、米国政府にとって協調介入の必要性が低下している可能性が
あるためだ。米国政府は当時、日本の長期金利上昇と円安が米国債市場に波及し、米金利を押し上げ
る経路を意識していたとみられる。足元では日米ともに長期金利の上昇が一服しており、日本発の市
場の混乱を防ぐ動機が弱まっている可能性は相応にある。今後、米国政府高官の発言などを機に、米
国が協調介入実施に慎重という見方が広がれば、市場の介入警戒感が和らぎかねない。その場合は、
投機筋のポジション調整を伴う形で、160円を上回って円安が進行するリスクに注意が必要だ。 
以上を踏まえ、2026年末にかけては、米利上げ観測が続き、150円台後半～160円近辺での円安水準
が続く展開を予想する。ただし、投機筋の円買いが巻き戻されたり、協調介入への警戒が和らいだり

すれば、164円近辺までの円安は十分に射程圏内に入る。為替介入が実施されれば1ドル＝150円程度ま
での一時的な円高は想定されるものの、その後は再び円安を試す動きが強まりやすいだろう。

［参考文献］ 
東深澤武史（2026）「160円台が「円安すぎ」ではない理由～「期待インフレ上昇」という新たな構造
変化」、みずほ総合研究所『みずほ経済 EXPRESS』、2026年7月29日 
東深澤武史ほか（2024）「円安が進むドル円の再評価～緩やかな円高予想も、日米金利や円需給を巡る
不確実性は円安リスクに」、みずほリサーチ＆テクノロジーズ『みずほリポート』、2024年4
月23日

1 この間、米国の期待インフレ率は概ね 2%台半ばで横ばい推移となっている。2026 年の中東情勢悪化を受けて急速な原油高が
進展した場面でも、期待インフレ率の急激な上昇は回避されている。背景には、米国の利上げ期待が急速に進んだことにある。
FRB が迅速に利上げを進めることで、先行きのインフレ率は安定するだろうという市場の期待が反映されていると解釈すること
ができる。 
2 実需要因も、円相場の趨勢を考えるうえで重要な要因である。足元の相場変動への寄与は限定的なため本文では詳述していな
いが、実需面の構造変化に関する分析は東深澤ほか（2024）を参照されたい。

漢唐(2404 TT) Buy

3Q26 獲利優於預期，客戶建廠需求暢旺，給 Buy 的投資建
議。 
投資建議： 3Q26 獲利優於預期，加上在手訂單金額約 1,939 億
元，有助於未來漢唐營運，給 Buy 的投資建議。 
漢唐 3Q26 獲利優於預期：漢唐 3Q26 營收 275.06 億元，
QoQ+5.47%；稅後純益 45.69 億元，QoQ+21.43%；稅 後 EPS 23.97
元。3Q26 稅後純益優於原先預估的 39.27 億元，主因毛利率優
於預期。 
漢唐目前在手訂單金額約 1,939 億元：漢唐 01~05/2026 年接單
合約金額約 674 億元，截至 05/2026 年已簽約未認列之金額(在
手訂單)約 1,939 億元。在手訂單約 24~36 個月可以認列大部分。
目前在手訂單金額的施工地區，有 2/3 在台灣，1/3 在海外。 
24~36 個月認列大部分的在手訂單可能是保守估計，2H26 在手
訂單金額可能還會再增加。 
漢唐對營運樂觀：漢唐表示目前建廠需求遠大於供給，因目前
人力短缺，尤其是在台灣，暫時沒辦法去接其他大型客戶的訂
單。由於 AI 與相關需求，現在客戶建廠的需求會延續到 2029
年，漢唐對整體產業的展望表示樂觀。 
群益預估漢唐 2026 年 /2027 年稅後 EPS 85.54 元 /117.99 元：群

### Page 2

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 13 日

2 群益投顧 
  ESG：  
永續環境是漢唐經營理念之綠色承諾，因應全球環保節能的需求，我們透過
工程設計、系統整合提升工程效率，減少工程過程中能耗。漢唐集成響應政
府綠色採購辦法，我們從原物料取得、工程施作、運輸配送、完工至客戶使
用階段，優先要求廠商提供低污染、可回收、再利用之環保產品，以達到廢
棄物減量之目標。 
漢唐集成根著台灣，從初創之施工安裝小公司成長為專業工程集成公司，有
賴於各地政通人和，因此，漢唐集成本著感恩的心回饋在地，不斷捐助弱勢
及支持培育英才，期望肩擔起企業之「社會責任」，讓台灣變得更美好。漢
唐股東會由全體股東組成，對公司重大事項進行決策，定期聽取董事會報
告，為公司最高決策機關；董事會為最高治理機關，董事會成員皆恪盡善良
管理人之注意義務，擘劃公司的經營政策與檢視財務績效，並確保公司營運
遵守各種法令；為完善公司治理運作、強化公司競爭力，董事會設置審計委
員會與薪資報酬委員會來健全董事會運作；董事會之下亦設有獨立之稽核
室，定期執行稽核業務並向審計委員會與董事會呈報稽核結果。 
公司簡介： 
漢唐集成成立於 1982 年，為一以「全面性整合服務」方式，專業提供高科
技產業建廠之系統集成服務公司；主要業務範圍包括高科技廠房各系統，包
括電腦、控制、無塵室、機電、電信系統之施作與整合服務。 
漢唐目前在手訂單金額約 1,939 億元： 
漢唐 01~05/2026 年接單合約金額約 674 億元，截至 05/2026 年已簽約未認
列之金額(在手訂單)約 1,939 億元。在手訂單約 24~36 個月可以認列大部
分。目前在手訂單金額的施工地區，有 2/3 在台灣，1/3 在海外。 
24~36 個月認列大部分的在手訂單可能是保守估計，2H26 在手訂單金額可
能還會再增加。 
漢唐未來還有先進封裝廠的訂單： 
大客戶於美國建廠的第三期工程的訂單目前漢唐已經有接到一部份，還沒
有認列到手訂單金額中，客戶的先進封裝廠的訂單還沒發包。第二期工程進
度希望在 2026 年認列約 85%，第三期工程的認列狀況要看客戶，未來還有
先進封裝廠的訂單。 
美國營收已超過台灣： 
漢唐沒有透露前兩大客戶的營收占比。2025 年營收占比_地區別：台灣 65%、
美國 25%、其他 9%。 1Q26 台灣 40%、美國 56%、其他 4%。美系記憶體廠
在台灣蓋無塵室，漢唐還是主要的供應商。 
漢唐對營運樂觀： 
漢唐表示目前建廠需求遠大於供給，因目前人力短缺，尤其是在台灣，暫時
沒辦法去接其他大型客戶的訂單。由於 AI 與相關需求，現在客戶建廠的需
求會延續到 2029 年，漢唐對整體產業的展望表示樂觀。

其他補充： 
漢唐的費用比較固定，管理費用包含員工酬勞。漢唐追求與客戶長期合作，
如果是原物料上漲導致成本增加，客戶可以接受漲價。 
目前漢唐在全球的員工人數約 1,700 人，美國、台灣都會再增加人力，之後
也是要看下包廠商，看是否能擴大供應鏈。

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 1

Voltronic Power (6409.TW)
What’s New from Taiwan Corporate Day: Online UPS Leads 2027E 
Growth; HVDC Moves Toward Qualification 
CITI'S TAKE 
Management expects online UPS to lead in 2027E, growing 10-15% y/y on 
sustained outsourcing and rising demand for high-power UPS, with offline 
up 5-10% y/y. Three tier 1 western vendor projects underpin Voltronic’s 
HVDC pipeline. The lead program targets qualification shipments in 1Q27E 
and mass production in 1H28E. Mgmt sees HVDC contributing 10% of group 
sales in 2028E. We lower our TP to NT$1,000 (26x 27E EPS) reflecting 
mostly lowered earnings due to higher input costs and ongoing inverter 
competition. Stay Buy in view of sustained UPS outsourcing, rising high-
power demand and advancing HVDC projects. 
HVDC progress: added one new project — M
gmt. noted currently there are three project under development and the progress 
remains on track. First project – Voltronic plans to ship PSUs in 1Q27E for customer 
qualification, start safety certification in 4Q27E and begin mass production in 
1H28E. Second project – the customer has requested a 499V, 1MW rack solution 
covering the full rack, including power distribution units, PSUs and BBUs. Voltronic 
expects to deliver a prototype in 1H27E. Third project – the customer recently re-
engaged Voltronic to design HVDC products after its initial interaction in Feb 2026. 
In addition to the Tier 1 UPS vendors, the mgmt. has also received inquiries from Tier 
2 UPS brands. The preliminary forecast for HVDC contribution is reaching 10% of 
group sales in 2028E. The company expects its existing facility in Taiwan to cover 
initial requirements and will assess expansion once customer volume forecasts firm 
up. 
UPS outlook: outsourcing and high-power demand support the growth — Online 
UPS sales rose 22% y/y in 3Q26 and mgmt. expects it to remain the growth driver in 
2027E with sales up 10-15% y/y. The ongoing outsourcing trend from the Tier 1 UPS 
vendors and rising demand for high-power online-UPS are the key drivers. High 
power UPS made up 7% of group sales in 3Q and carried a 31% GPM. Mgmt. expects 
high-power UPS to reach c.10% of group sales in 2027E. Offline UPS sales grew 27% 
y/y in 3Q26 (helped by a low base), and mgmt. expects it to grow 5-10% y/y in 2027E.

### Page 3

Voltronic Power (6409.TW)
24 November 2026 Citi Research
3
Inverter: portfolio shift underway yet margins likely remain under pressure —
Mgmt expects inverter sales to be flat y/y in 2027E after a 35% y/y dip in 3Q26,
impacted mostly by the Middle East conflict. Voltronic began to phase out low-
margin products in 3Q26 (off-grid) and is prioritizing higher-power, commercial-
grade products where customers require more customization. It is also expanding in

NA and India. India now accounts for 20% of inverter sales, supported by residential
subsidies. Intense price competition from Chinese suppliers, limited ability to pass
through higher input costs and the unfavorable geo mix are likely to keep margins
under pressure during the transition in our view.
Implications — We trim our 2026-2028E EPS estimates 9-15% mainly to reflect
lower GPM assumptions in view of elevated input costs and continued inverter

pricing and mix pressure. We trim our TP to NT$,1000 from NT$1,050 to reflect lower
earnings which is based on a 26x 2027E, on par with its historical average. We stay
Buy in view of continued outsourcing by western UPS vendors which supports share
gains and a richer mix of online and higher-power UPS supporting growth and
profitability. Beyond UPS, Voltronic’s relationships with Tier 1 power vendors and
experience in high-voltage systems position it to capture more content value as

HVDC programs move toward commercialization.

Voltronic Power (6409.TW)
24 November 2026 Citi Research
5
Voltronic Power
Company description
Established in 2008 and listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange in 2014,
Voltronic is a Taiwan-based design and manufacturing service (DMS) provider
specializing in uninterrupted power supplies (UPS) and power-conversion
products. Voltronic adopts a pure-play ODM business model, which avoids
direct competition with customers and has helped the company establish

long-term relationships with most global Tier-1 UPS brands. The company is
headquartered in Taipei and operates manufacturing facilities in Taiwan,
China and Vietnam.
Investment strategy
We have a Buy rating on Voltronic, supported by an earnings recovery,
structural UPS outsourcing and emerging opportunities in AIDC power
infrastructure. Its customization capabilities and longstanding relationships
with western power vendors position it to capture additional outsourcing

orders and expand into higher power products. HVDC power racks offer a L-T
growth opportunity and potential re-rating catalyst. We view 2026E as
earnings trough, with a richer online UPS mix and operating leverage driving a
28% earnings rebound in 2027E.
Valuation
We value Voltronic using a forward target P/E of 26x our 2027E EPS to derive a
target price of NT$1000, broadly in line with its long term average forward P/E
since 2013. We believe forward P/E is appropriate given the company’s

established earnings base and the importance of product mix and operating
leverage to its profitability.
Risks
Key downside risks to our Buy rating and target price include: 1) Slower UPS
demand or outsourcing gain. 2) Less favorable product mix and margin
pressure. 3) Persistent PV inverter competition. 4) Weaker macro economy

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

精測(6510 TT) Buy

精測受惠 HPC 測試需求，2027 年 營 收、獲 利 皆 可 望 再 創 新
高 ， 給 Buy 的 投 資 評 等 。

收 、 獲 利 皆 可 望 再 創 新 高 ，給 Buy 的 投 資 評 等 。  
精測 3Q26 營 收 、 獲 利 皆 創 單 季 新 高 ： 精測 3Q26 營收 19.05 億
元，QoQ+16.17%\YoY+53.35%；毛 利 率 57.26%、營 益 率 35.21%；
稅後純益 5.51 億 元，QoQ+11.61%\YoY+100.03%；稅 後 EPS 16.76
元。3Q26 營 收 、 獲 利 皆 創 單 季 新 高 ， 雖 然 因 新 產 品 開 始 認 列 營
收 ， 毛 利 率 略 為 季 減 ， 但 獲 利 成 長 還 是 大 於 營 收 成 長 。  
精 測 有 TPU 與 AI5 商機： 精 測 表 示 ， 美 系 客 戶 的 TPU 供 應 商
維 持 既 有 架 構 ， 預 期 在  TPU8 專 案 中 將 扮 演 一 定 角 色 ， 不 過 目
前 尚 未 正 式 發 包，相 關 專 案 預 計 於 2H26 啟 動。此 外，針 對 另 一
客戶的 AI5 專 案， 依 客 戶 規 劃 時 程， 預 計 06/2026 進 行 工 程 卡
交 付 ， 並 預 計 於 2026 年底至 2027 年 初 進 入 量 產 。  
精 測 新 產 能 持 續 開 出：2026 年 營 運 逐 季 成 長 趨 勢 明 確，3Q26 營
收 有 機 會 雙 位 數 季 增 ， 08/2026 、 03/2027 會 有 比 較 大 的 產 能 開
出 。 目 前 交 期 約 5~8 周 ， 新 產 能 客 戶 驗 證 時 間 約 需 一 季 ， 2027
年 營 運 健 康 成 長 。

2 群益投顧 
  ESG：  
在 環 境 保 護 方 面 ， 中 華 精 測 於 2022 年 開 始 自 主 性 導 入 溫 室 氣 體 盤 查 作 業 ，
除 了 計 算 碳 使 用 總 量 外，也 了 解 如 何 節 能 及 尋 找 替 代 能 源，並 推 動 各 項 環 保
節 能 減 碳 措 施、減 少 溫 室 氣 體 排 放 量 等，以 期 減 緩 與 調 適 氣 候 變 遷 所 帶 來 之
營 運 衝 擊 。  
在 社 會 責 任 方 面 ， 中 華 精 測 遵 守 相 關 法 規 及 國 際 人 權 公 約 ， 持 續 營 造 友 善 、
平 等、自 由 的 工 作 環 境，對 於 提 升 員 工 福 祉 不 遺 餘 力，且 不 定 期 舉 辦 家 庭 活
動 及 獎 勵 同 仁 結 婚 、 生 育 、 旅 遊 津 貼 等 員 工 福 利 。  
在 公 司 治 理 營 運 績 效 方 面 ， 中 華 精 測 連 續 兩 年 於 公 司 治 理 評 鑑 排 名 前 5%，
並於 2022 年 榮 獲 上 櫃 公 司 治 理 評 鑑  Top5%之 佳 績，秉 持 誠 信 正 直 原 則，定
期 宣 導 及 教 育 訓 練 內 線 交 易、誠 信 廉 潔 等 法 律 知 識，確 保 公 司 員 工 遵 守 誠 信
經 營 守 則 之 落 實 。  
公 司 簡 介 ：  
中 華 精 測 科 技 股 份 有 限 公 司 (6510 TT)設 立 於 08/2005，總 部 及 生 產 工 廠 皆 位
於 桃 園 平 鎮，為 中 華 電 信 所 屬 關 係 企 業，前 身 為 中 華 電 信 研 究 所 內 部 之 高 速
PCB 團 隊 ，2001 年 起 開 始 嘗 試 研 發 設 計 測 試 載 板 (Load board) 及 探 針 卡 載 板
(Probe PCB) 。  
中 華 精 測 主 要 從 事 晶 圓 測 試 板 、 IC 測 試 板 、 技 術 服 務 與 其 他 等 業 務 ， 提 供
半導體產業測試所需的介面板服務，用於晶圓測試之探針卡專用印刷電路
板 (Probe PCB) ， 及 用 於 IC 測 試 之 載 版 (Load Board) 。  
中 華 電 信 (2412 TT) 持股中 華 投 資 公 司 約 89.0% ； 中 華 投 資 持 股 精 測 約
33.6%；聯發科(2454 TT)於 2015 年，透 過 旗 下 翔 發 投 資 公 司 入 股 中 華 精 測，
目 前 持 股 約 0.5%。  
精 測 的 台 灣 同 業 包 含 旺 矽 (6223 TT)、穎崴(6515 TT)、 雍 智 科 技 (6683 TT)…
等。精 測 的 客 戶 包 含 美 系 HPC 公 司、台 系 IC 設 計 公 司、美 系 IC 設 計 公 司，
台 系 晶 圓 代 工 廠 …等。 
營 收 占 比 _產 品 別 ：  
產 品 目 前 毛 利 率 排 序 ： 整 組 探 針 卡 >FT 用的 loadboard>SLT 板 / 老化板。 
晶 圓 測 試 卡 包 含 PCB 與 整 組 探 針 卡 。

3 群益投顧 
資 料 來 源 ： 精 測 法 說 會 簡 報 。  
營 收 占 比 _產 品 應 用 別 ：

資 料 來 源 ： 精 測 法 說 會 簡 報 。  
精測 4Q26 毛 利 率 可 能 季 減 ：  
精 測 之 前 說 3Q26 毛 利 率 區 間 為 50~55%，但 實 際 毛 利 率 優 於 預 期，因 09/2026
才 開 始 出 毛 利 率 較 差 的 老 化 板，所 以 對 3Q26 毛 利 率 影 響 有 限，精 測 估 4Q26
毛利率 50~55%， 但 還 是 要 看 產 品 組 合 。  
精 測 估 2027 年 營 收 、 獲 利 雙 位 數 成 長 ：  
精測估 1Q27 營 收 將 介 於 3Q26~4Q26 間，精 測 表 示 以 目 前 訂 單 的 狀 況，2027
年 營 收 維 持 50%的 成 長 ， EPS 的 成 長 也 是 可 以 期 待 。  
整 組 探 針 卡 於 往 年 的 合 併 營 收 占 比 約 20~30%，精 測 2027 年 挑 戰 占 比 30%，
但 也 要 看 其 他 產 品 的 成 長 性 。  
現 在 由 於 營 收 規 模 成 長，精 測 建 議 費 用 率 預 估 為 20~25%，用 於 研 發、留 才、
與 國 際 大 廠 的 合 作 等 。  
TPU 工 程 驗 證 產 品 預 計 11/2026 開 始 出 貨 ：  
傳聞 TPU 產 品 因 封 裝 問 題 ， 量 產 時 間 遞 延 ， 精 測 表 示 工 程 驗 證 產 品 的 出 貨
沒 有 遞 延 ， 量 產 時 間 預 計 會 是 1H27， 量 產 的 數 量 還 沒 有 確 定 。  
TPU 專 案，精 測 將 於 11/2026 開 始 出 貨 工 程 驗 證 用 的 整 組 探 針 卡，之 後 量 產
的 數 量 還 沒 確 定 。 記 憶 體 用 的 整 組 探 針 卡 也 會 於 2026 年 送 樣 。  
美 系 車 用 晶 片 大 廠 的 訂 單 ， 精 測 也 於 2026 年 開 始 出 貨 工 程 驗 證 的 產 品 ， 預
計將於 2027 年 量 產 ， 之 後 量 產 的 數 量 還 沒 確 定 。 工 程 驗 證 結 束 ， 到 正 式 量
產 通 常 需 要 1~2 季。 
除了 TPU 專 案 以 外 ， 精 測 也 積 極 驗 證 北 美 、 韓 國 客 戶 ， 精 測 估 2027 年 的 產
能 與 稼 動 率 皆 能 維 持 高 檔 。  
精 測 有 發 CB， 三 廠 產 能 預估 4Q28 開出：  
精測 07/29 重 訊 決 議 發 行 CB， 預 計 募 資 20 億 元 ， 主 要 用 於 新 廠 的 工 程 款 ，

8 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】  
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 ≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15%≤首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 <35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 <15%

中立(Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等  
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整  
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】  
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較 3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為 四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等 係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。   
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」 及 「 區 間 操 作 」 均有 upside 目 標 價 。 差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。  
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ：  
1. 達 目 標 價 。  
2. 雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。  
3. 推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 ， 亦 將 出 具 降 評 報 告 。

【免責聲明】 
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

## Document 241: [005380] 현대차(005380) 공급 회복에 신차 사이클이 더해진다현대차(005380) 공급 회복에 신차 사이클이 더해진다공급 회복에 신차 사이클이_7a155553

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005380] 현대차(005380) 공급 회복에 신차 사이클이 더해진다현대차(005380) 공급 회복에 신차 사이클이 더해진다공급 회복에 신차 사이클이_7a155553.pdf

### Page 1

기아 (000270/KS) 
라인업 확대의 성과와 주주환원에 주목 
2027 년 영업이익 7.96 조 원 전망 
2027 년 실적은 매출액 124.3 조 원(YoY +1.0%), 영업이익 7.96 조 원(YoY -
5.3%, OPM 6.4%), 지배주주순이익 6.97 조 원(YoY -0.2%)으로 전망한다. 우호적
이었던 환율 효과가 약해지는 가운데, 높아진 원재료 투입가격과 유럽을 중심으로 한 
판매 경쟁도 수익성에 부담으로 남는다. 판매를 지키기 위한 인센티브 확대까지 감안하
면 물량 증가가 그대로 이익 증가로 이어지기는 어렵다. 다만 이미 투입한 신차의 연간 
판매 기여와 미국, 유럽의 라인업 확대가 비용 부담을 일부 상쇄하면서, 하반기에는 전
년 동기 대비 증익으로 돌아설 것으로 본다.

지역별로 갖춘 제품, PBV 로 넓어지는 고객 
2027 년에는 이미 출시한 신차의 판매를 늘려 제품과 설비에 투자한 성과를 매출과 이
익으로 연결하는 일이 중요하다. 미국에서는 텔루라이드 등 주력 SUV 와 확대된 하이
브리드 차종으로 수요에 대응한다. 전기차 전환을 서두르지 않는 고객에게도 선택지를 
제공하며, 이 가운데 현지 생산 차종은 완성차 수입에 따른 관세 부담을 줄이는 데도 도
움이 된다. 유럽에서는 EV2·EV4 등 전기차와 신형 셀토스로 판매 기회를 넓힌다. 기
존 슬로바키아 공장을 활용해 전기차를 공급할 수 있지만, 중국 업체와의 경쟁 속에서 
판촉 부담을 관리하는 과제도 남아 있다.

PBV 는 PV5 에 이어 PV7 을 투입하며 물류 및 운송업체의 용도별 수요에 대응할 계
획이다. 차량 크기와 적재 수요에 맞춘 제품에 판매와 정비망을 더하면 고객과의 거래
를 넓힐 수 있다. 미국 하이브리드의 판매 확대, 유럽 전기차의 안착, PBV 의 고객 확보
는 각각 이익에 기여하는 속도가 다르다. 따라서 판매 증가와 함께 제품 구성의 변화, 
신규 차종의 초기 비용이 수익성에 미치는 영향을 살펴야 한다.

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 12 만 원으로 커버리지 개시 
투자의견 매수, 목표주가 12 만 원으로 커버리지를 개시한다. 당사 12 개월 선행 EPS 에 
최근 1 년 평균 P/E 6.5 배를 적용했다. 연간 감익 부담은 남아 있지만, 지역별 수요에 
대응할 제품과 생산기반은 확대돼 있다. 낮아진 밸류에이션 부담을 감안하면, 이미 갖
춘 라인업의 판매 성과와 총주주환원율 35% 이상의 정책에 주목해야 한다는 판단이
다. 
2026-11-25

3Q26 Review: “조삼모사” Again  
3Q26P 연결 실적은 매출액 6조 943억원(YoY -6.0%), 영업이익 4,520억원(YoY -46.2%, OPM 
7.4%)을 기록하며 컨센서스 대비 연결 매출액(7.5조원)과 영업이익(1.16조원) 모두 대폭 하회했다. 주
요 자회사가 양호한 실적을 기록했음에도 불구하고, 지상방산 실적이 매우 부진했다. 지상방산은 매출
액 1조 2,455억원(YoY -41.0%), 영업이익 217억원(YoY -96.2%, OPM 1.7%)을 기록했는데, 1)3Q 
생산 물량이 4Q 매출 인식되는 흐름 속, 고정비 부담이 컸었고, 2)낮은 수출 비중(3Q 48%, 2Q 58%)
과 국내외 사업의 부진한 믹스(국내 BEP 수준, 해외 OPM 11% 추정), 3)비우호적인 원/달러 환율, 4)
대전 사업장 사고 복구 및 기타 일회성 비용(300억원+160억원, 총 460억원) 등이 반영되었다.   
대전 사업장 정상화 시점과 사업 차질 영향은? 
대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 중 정상화될 예정이며, 보은과 여수 공장에서 생산 보완을 위한 조치가 진
행 중인 것으로 소통했다. 또한 대전 사업장은 국내 물량 위주이며, 수출 납기(천무, 천궁-II 추진체 
등)에는 지장이 없을 것이라고 언급했지만, 기타 사업장 램프업을 위한 인건비 증가 등, 수익성 악화는 
불가피할 것으로 예상된다.  
15%대의 매출 성장 가이던스는 달성이 가능할까? 
보수적으로 접근하는 것이 합리적이라는 판단이다. 당사는 ‘26E 지상방산 매출액을 일부 ‘27~28E로 
이연하면서 -6.1% 하향 조정했다. 사측은 폴란드 EC1 천무 인도 가이던스를 기존 40문에서 60문으
로 상향 조정하는 등 납품 속도가 예정대비 빨라진 것을 언급했다. 다만, 천무 EC1에서 미사일 물량 
비중이 높은 것으로 파악되기 때문에 생산 지연 가능성과 수출입 데이터 상 12월 출하 물량의 매출 인
식 이월 가능성을 반영했다. ‘27E는 폴란드 후속 사업과 루마니아 K9, 호주 레드백 등이 양산 페이즈
에 진입하며 성장세가 지속될 전망이다.    
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 1,480,000원 유지 
실적 쇼크와는 별개로, 미국 K9MH 시제, 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등 수주는 서프라이즈라고 판
단된다. 분기말 수주잔고는 35조원(수출 비중 74%, 스페인 미포함)으로 중장기 성장성에 대한 이견은 
없다. 불가피한 분기 실적 변동으로 동사의 미래 가치를 예단하는 것은 시기상조라는 판단이다.

## Document 273: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(373220) 
LG 에너지솔루션 
2026.10.12

에너지/인프라/배터리 황성현, Ph.D. 
6878 / tjdgus2009@eugenefn.com

보상금 제외해도 호실적 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 530,000 원(상향) 
 현재주가 401,000 원(10/8)

 [투자의견] 당사는 LG에너지솔루션에 대해 실적 추정치 상향을 반영하여 목
표주가를 530,000원으로 13% 상향하고, 투자의견 BUY를 유지한다. 
3Q26부터 ESS 물량 증가에 따른 가동률 상승 효과가 본격적으로 실적에 
반영되기 시작했으며, 4Q26에도 AMPC 제외 흑자 기조를 이어갈 전망이
다. 전사 가동률은 3분기 65%까지 상승한 것으로 파악되며, 이에 따른 펀
더멘털 개선이 본격화되고 있다고 판단한다. 
 [3Q26 잠정 실적 Review] 매출액 9.6조원(+28%qoq, +59%yoy), 영업이
익 7,560억원(+567%qoq, +26%yoy)을 기록하며 당사 추정을 큰 폭으로 
상회했다. 북미 생산보조금(AMPC)을 제외한 매출액은 9.2조원, 영업이익은 
3,391억원(영업이익률 3.7%)으로, 2분기(-1,277억원, -1.7%) 대비 흑자 
전환에 성공했다. ① 원통형은 테슬라향 물량 호조에 힘입어 영업이익 
2,010억원(영업이익률 8.1%)을 달성한 것으로 추정된다. ② EV는 유럽 폴
란드 공장의 가동률 상승과 GM 얼티엄의 8월 가동 개시로 매출이 증가했
다. 여기에 완성차 업체 보상금 3,000억원이 반영되며 영업이익 1,090억
원(영업이익률 2.5%)을 기록한 것으로 추정된다. ③ ESS는 매출액이 50% 
이상 급증하며 실적 서프라이즈를 견인했다. AMPC 포함 영업이익은 4,460
억원(영업이익률 16.0%)으로 추정된다. 2분기 매출 성장률 +17%qoq에 
이어 3분기 성장세가 더욱 가팔라졌다는 점이 긍정적이다. 
 [4Q26 Preview] 매출액 8.0조원(-17%qoq, +30%yoy), 영업이익 6,790
억원(-10%qoq, 흑전yoy)으로 추정한다. 4분기 실적은 ESS 수익성 개선 
속도와 완성차 업체 보상금 반영 규모에 따라 변동할 전망이다. 동사는 기존 
가이던스를 통해 하반기 북미 ESS 생산량이 상반기 대비 2배 증가할 것으
로 제시한 바 있다. 3분기 ESS는 AMPC를 제외하더라도 양호한 수익성을 
기록한 것으로 추정된다. 향후 팩 생산 병목 해소와 북미 공장 가동 안정화
에 따른 고정비 부담 완화가 이어지며 추가적인 손익 개선이 가능할 전망이
다. EV 역시 GM 얼티엄 생산 확대와 유럽 공장 가동률 상승이 지속되며 
AMPC 제외 적자 규모를 점진적으로 축소할 것으로 예상한다. 
 [투자포인트] 9월 미시간 랜싱 공장의 개소식이 개최됐다. 랜싱 공장은 당초 
EV용 파우치 배터리 생산거점으로 계획됐으나 ESS 중심으로 역할이 재편
됐으며, 내년에는 10GWh 이상의 각형 LFP 생산라인이 추가될 예정이다. 
2027년에는 신규 대규모 증설보다 기존 북미 생산거점의 가동률 상승과 
ESS 생산량 확대를 통한 수익성 개선이 핵심이 될 전망이다. 북미 전력망 
접속 지연과 AI 데이터센터의 부하 변동성 확대에 따라 Stand-alone ESS, 
BTM, UPS, BBU 수요가 증가하면서 신규 수주도 지속 확대될 것으로 예상
한다. 특히 동사의 Non-PFE 요건 충족과 SI· O&M을 아우르는 End-to-
End 솔루션 역량이 북미 ESS 시장에서 경쟁력으로 작용할 전망이다.

시가총액(십억원) 93,834 
 발행주식수(천주) 234,000 
 52 주 최고 527,000 원 
  최저 290,500 원

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에 동 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율 
종목추천 및 업종추천 투자기간: 12 개월  (추천기준일 종가대비 추천종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함) 당사 투자의견 비율(%)

## Document 278: AI is boosting business creations both in the US and Europe (2026-11-04)

4 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
AI IS BOOSTING BUSINESS CREATIONS BOTH IN THE US AND EUROPE
Stéphane Colliac
The significant increase in business creations since mid-2025 in the United States, France and Germany – and, to a lesser extent, in the United
Kingdom – is continuing. Across the board, this trend is being driven by the information and communication sector and mostly by AI. It is
spreading to other business services, while developments in consumer services, industry and construction are more mixed. However, in both

the United States and France, the trend is broadening, suggesting that AI is facilitating business creation across all sectors.

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 4 November 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A noticeable boom in the United States, France, and Germany, but not quite as pronounced in the United Kingdom
The four economies for which we have comparable data (the United States, France, Germany and the United Kingdom) have all
seen, to varying degrees, an increase in the number of new business creations. While the timing varies across these countries,

most of them are enjoying strong momentum. In the United States, there has been a noticeable acceleration since the second
quarter of 2025, which has continued to strengthen. Germany has followed a similar trajectory, moving from slight declines at
the start of 2024 to sharp increases. France is experiencing the same trend but with some specific features, as momentum has
picked up significantly after months of slowdown (due to the uncertainty following the dissolution of the National Assembly) and is

accelerating across most sectors in 2026. In the United Kingdom, the trend is less pronounced and the increase may not last.
Business services, and AI in particular, are driving this momentum
In most of these four countries, business creations are rising significantly in the information and communication sector, which is
directly linked to AI. In the United States, Germany and France, there has been a pronounced trend which is gaining momentum.
In the United Kingdom, the AI sector is also seeing an increase, but it is significantly less pronounced. However, this momentum is

not limited to this sector. It is spreading to many services sectors, including finance, real estate and business services. Growth has
been sustained since mid-2025 in the United States and Germany, and since the third quarter of 2025 in France.
The picture for consumer services is more mixed. In the United States, trade, accommodation and catering have experienced major
increases in creations since mid-2025. In France, these sectors are in positive territory, particularly trade, where the increase in

business creations has recently accelerated. In Germany, these same sectors fell into negative territory in 2026 following a rather
mixed year in 2025.
The situation is even more mixed for manufacturing and construction. In the United States, these sectors are also part of the
overall positive momentum in the third quarter of 2026. This shows that AI does not only benefit sectors closely linked to it
(notably the digital technology sector) but that it makes it easier to start a business across the entire economy (by simplifying

administrative procedures in particular). In France, the upturn in business creations in the construction sector suggests that this
same trend is at play, even as activity in the sector continues to contract.
Therefore, the trend is more widespread in the United States and is becoming increasingly prevalent in France. It is driven by
specific sectors of the services industry in Germany, while it is significantly more subdued in the United Kingdom.

stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

## Document 311: Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-26)

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Oil Price Dynamics Remain Key to the Outlook
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Inflation, interest rates and equity market dynamics have changed materially since mid-
August, as widening attacks on Middle East and Russian crude oil and refining capacity
worsened supply and price conditions. Indeed, with the oil market already strained, an
early-September attack on a major Saudi Arabian export pipeline sharply raised concerns

about a protracted and deepening oil crisis. While Middle East crude exports eventually
managed to significantly rebound, increased security risks and freight costs raised
physical crude prices.
Against this backdrop, the spot Brent price surged from $92/barrel in mid-August
back into a $115 to $135/barrel price range until recently. Reflecting unusual market
tightness, other North Sea oil grades were recently trading even higher, up to $160/
barrel, according to the International Energy Agency (IEA). Even as it cooled from $135

to $115/barrel between October 2 and October 20, spot Brent is still almost double
year-ago levels, and so are many diesel prices. The AAA average U.S. retail diesel price
recently hit a new record and was 74% higher year-over-year (YoY) as of October 20.
With insufficient regional supply, the New York Harbor wholesale diesel price is 130%
higher from a year ago, according to the Energy Information Administration (EIA).
Indeed, sharply higher oil prices and a growing diesel shortage have put the global diesel

market under extreme strain. The U.S., for example, has little distillate cushion and
limited spare refining capacity. The distillate inventory (diesel and fuel oil) has decreased
from 37 days of demand coverage in January to just 26 days by early October, according
to the EIA. This 30% drop brought the supply buffer to the bottom of its historical
range. With limited refining capacity and reliance on imports for about 10% of regional
consumption, East Coast distillate inventories are the lowest since 1990, for example,

and are expected to remain below their 5-year average into late 2027, according to EIA
data. More broadly, according to the IEA’s October Oil Market Report (OMR), “oil market
balances are forecast to remain in deficit through the end of the year, with constrained
middle distillate supplies likely to persist well into 2027.”
Thus, although wage growth/labor demand do not appear inflationary, the oil crisis
risks continuing to feed “core” inflation. According to the Federal Reserve’s Beige Book1

covering precisely the mid-August to early-October period of heightened oil-market
turmoil, “higher oil prices permeated all District reports,” with firms noting that fuel
surcharges were passing on increased costs throughout the supply chain. The 22% rise
in airfares over the past year alone contributed around 0.2 percentage point (pp) to the
2.6% 12-month increase in the “core” consumer price index (CPI) for September. Aside
from transportation costs effects, the spike in diesel prices also portends high food

prices with a few-month lag given planting/harvesting cost considerations.
By solidifying expectations for higher Fed interest rates to fight inflation, the surge in oil
prices has contributed to the spike in long-term Treasury yields this year. According to an
October 13, 2026, Empirical Research Partners report, the Iran-war oil crisis is estimated
to have contributed about 0.50 pp to the roughly 2.40 pp increase in the real U.S. long-
term Treasury rate from 2020 to early October (Exhibit 1A). According to the report, this

oil-price effect accounts for “much of the rise in real rates since February 27, 2026...”
On the positive side, while most other underpinnings for higher rates compared to the
pre-pandemic decade are structural (Exhibit 1A), the oil-price-related effect on interest
rates is reversible, implying that a resolution to the current oil crisis has the potential to
help lower both inflation and interest rates, relieving some of the current headwinds to
growth and asset prices.

Indeed, while much of the increase in interest rates since the pandemic reflects stronger,
more normal economic growth, and has thus been accompanied by surging corporate
1 Summary of Commentary on Current Economic Conditions by Federal Reserve District, Federal Reserve, October 2026.

3 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
earnings and risk-asset prices, worsening oil-market conditions since mid-summer and
associated spike in yields have been accompanied by a loss of momentum in U.S. equity
prices (Exhibit 1B). Given their higher sensitivity to energy price and interest rates shocks,
Small-caps lost relatively more momentum than Large-caps during this period.
The Large-cap rally also faded, though, as oil and interest rate headwinds widened stock-

level return dispersion, with roughly 40% of constituent stocks in bear market territory
(down 20% or more). Still, buoyed by the Technology sector as well as by expectations
of conflict resolution, the market-capitalization weighted S&P 500 Index has mainly
consolidated just below its peak. While the equal-weighted S&P 500 Index stumbled more,
it was just about 3% off its peak as of October 20.
While the economy remains resilient and profit expectations remain strong, persistent

oil market turmoil makes the macro backdrop more fragile. For example, the Beige Book
not only highlights concern about spreading fuel-related inflation, as noted above, but its
language also suggests that economic activity lost some momentum since its mid-July
to late-August report. Overall, it describes growth as slight to moderate, with generally
moderate manufacturing growth except for robust activity in the energy-heavy Dallas
District. Little growth is reported in other sectors overall, with most Districts seeing little

to no change in consumer spending, for example.
Employment was described as stable to little changed, with generally adequate labor supply
except for certain specialized trade workers. Five of twelve Districts note some reports of
businesses cutting staff through attrition, delaying hiring or anticipating less job creation
as a result of AI-driven productivity gains. Most Districts reported that business maintained
a positive outlook for coming months, though “expectations tend to be for slight growth,

weighted toward manufacturing and burdened by persistent uncertainty.”
The September employment and retail sales reports seem to validate the anecdotal Beige
Book evidence. Also, the 0.4% annualized manufacturing production growth over the July to
September period (and 1.1% YoY, according to the Federal Reserve Broad), while welcome,
is hardly an indication of booming conditions. Against a backdrop of rising electricity prices,
wage growth lagging inflation, and higher gasoline prices broadly offsetting this year’s tax

cut benefits, real consumer spending is unlikely to accelerate much absent stronger labor
demand and real wage growth. A resolution to the oil crisis that brings economic relief and
tempers interest rates is thus critical for continued expansion in 2027, in our view.

Investment Implications
Portfolio maintenance is a key
component of successful investing.
It’s time for investors to assess their
portfolios—or look under the hood.
MARKET VIEW
Six Questions Investors Should Be Asking Themselves Right Now
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
After four years of rising equity prices, investors have plenty to celebrate. Despite

inflation scares, geopolitical tensions, trade wars, rising interest rates and breathless
predictions around AI, the S&P 500 has returned some 131% (total returns) since the
bull market in equities commenced in October 2022. Corporate earnings have generally
exceeded expectations; AI has ignited a historic investment boom, and America’s
technology giants have powered the market to new heights.
We believe the bull will rumble on into 2027. We also believe that now is the time for

investors to take stock of their portfolios, since bull markets have a way of changing the
composition of portfolios. In addition, investors should not assume that the investment
strategies that worked well in the past four years will necessarily deliver comparable
results during the next four. Below are six questions investors should be asking
themselves right now:
Number One: How concentrated is my portfolio? Many investors believe they are
invested in the stock market when, in fact, they are invested in a handful of companies—

specifically AI-related names that have soared and dominated the index over the past
few years. Information technology now accounts for roughly 40% of the market cap of
the S&P 500, while the top 10 companies represent a share of 38.4%.
As the Financial Times recently noted, U.S. equity and credit markets have become so
increasingly concentrated in AI-related groups that “more than two-thirds of companies
in the Russell 1000 index of large U.S. groups are linked to AI, either directly or

indirectly or because the technology is becoming embedded in their business models.”2
Meanwhile, Big Technology hyperscalers and companies that are beneficiaries of the AI
spending boom make up about 16% of the U.S. high-grade bond market after a huge
borrowing binge, making the grouping the largest in the investment-grade bond market.2
The upshot: Investors that have been rewarded by concentration need to ask themselves
what percentage of their wealth is tied to the all-encompassing AI trade and consider

what would happen if mega-cap tech leadership falters.
Number Two: How diversified is my portfolio? While concentration asks whether
investors own too much of a few assets, diversification asks whether investors own enough
of different assets that behave differently when economic cycles or financial conditions
change. A portfolio containing hundreds of U.S. Equities via exchange-traded funds, mutual
funds and other assets may appear diversified. But if those companies are overwhelmingly

exposed to the same economic cycle, interest rate environment, or technology theme,
the diversification benefits are diluted. Case in point: While we remain constructive on
Emerging Market (EM) Equities, investors need to understand that a significant driver of
EM returns has come from AI-driven markets like Taiwan and South Korea.
Today, true diversification means considering exposure to hard assets (commodities, real
estate), EM mining and agricultural leaders, EM bonds and currencies, China bio-technology,

Small-cap international, and Private Equity and Hedge Funds for qualified investors.
Number Three: How tax-efficient is my portfolio? Bull markets create wealth—in
addition to tax liabilities. The good news is that after four years of rising equity returns,
investors are sitting on sizable, unrealized capital gains. The bad news: Market gains
can feel like a financial straitjacket, making it difficult to rebalance portfolios without
triggering significant tax liabilities.

Against this backdrop, now is the time for investors to review where the assets are
held—distinguishing between taxable and tax-advantaged accounts, and the attendant tax
ramifications. The appropriate mix depends on individual circumstances, including future
2 “Investors seek shelter from heightened exposure to AI”, The Financial Times, October 6, 2026.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
withdrawal taxes. It is also time to consider tax-loss strategies, charitable donations of
securities and other tactics and strategies since in the end, the measure of investment
success isn’t simply how much money investors make. It’s how much they keep.
Number Four: Is it time to take profits?* There is a peculiarity to bull markets: The
more money investors make, the more the attendant tax liabilities. Tax liabilities, in turn,

can discourage selling, and the reluctance to sell can leave investors dangerously exposed
to yesterday’s leaders and compound concentration risks. That brings up an emotionally
difficult decision: When should investors sell their leaders or take some chips off the table?
The question is notably relevant today, given four years of substantial Equity gains.
Beyond tax considerations, investors need to understand that profit-taking can create
financial flexibility. Realized after-tax gains can replenish cash reserves, fund near-

term spending needs, reduce leverage or most importantly, provide capital to rebalance
portfolios in other areas of the market. In general, the goal of taking profits isn’t to get
out of the market—it is to ensure that after years of accumulated gains, these proceeds
are redeployed and redistributed across various asset classes that continue to support
and underpin long-term wealth accumulation.
Number Five: How well is my portfolio positioned for a new interest rate

regime? For much of the past quarter century, investors operated in a world of copious
liquidity, declining interest rates and relatively cheap capital. The era of capital abundance
favored stocks over bonds, but that era is over—we are shifting to a world of capital
scarcity, or intense competition for capital and the attendant hike in the cost of capital.
In this world, higher rates weigh the most on long-duration assets. Positioning for this
regime favors companies with strong balance sheets and cash flow over firms with heavy

debt or refinancing needs. High-quality bonds are more attractive in this environment;
sovereign debt risks are concentrated more overseas (i.e., France) than the U.S.
Number Six: How well is my portfolio positioned for the next shock? Shocks
happen. If the past four years have taught investors anything, it is that the unexpected
should be the expected. This decade alone has produced gut-wrenching market moves
related to a global pandemic, a ground war in Europe, the reconfiguration of global

supply chains, epic technological change and a war in the Middle East. Through it all, U.S.
Equities have trended higher but hardly in a linear fashion.
The next shocks could emerge in the global bond market, where mounting government
debt and higher borrowing costs have triggered mounting volatility. It could emerge from
disappointing returns on massive AI investments. Or it could come from a geopolitical
event, another energy shock or any other unforeseen event. Investors can’t predict the

next shock, but they can prepare for it. So now is the time for investors to stress test
their portfolios against a potential shock, examining how a steep downdraft in Equities
or spike in bond yields would affect their retirement plans, liquidity needs and other
financial goals and objectives.
Exhibit 2: Six Questions to Stress Test Your Portfolio.
The six questions What investors should examine Portfolio diagnostic
1. How concentrated am I? Mega-cap technology exposure and overlapping holdings % of portfolio in top 10 stocks

2. Am I truly diversified? Asset allocation, geographic mix and correlated exposures Current equity/bond mix versus target
3. How tax-efficient am I? Unrealized gains, asset location and tax-loss harvesting Embedded capital gains and estimated tax cost of rebalancing
4. Is it time to take profits? Portfolio drift, oversized winners and valuations Positions exceeding target allocation
5. Am I ready for higher interest rates? Duration, leverage, refinancing risks and cash flows Bond duration and exposure to highly leveraged companies

6. Am I prepared for the next shock? Liquidity, downside exposure and portfolio resilience Estimated loss in a 20% equity selloff
Source: Chief Investment Office. Data as of October 22, 2026.
* “Pop the Champagne,” Portfolio Insights, Chief Investment Office, August 2026.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are rising credit spreads signaling about the broader market environment?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
Rising French bond yields with a sharp rise in spreads above German bunds, mixed
with AI buildout concerns, are creating nervousness across the capital markets. This
nervousness centers around the ability to fund deficits, the potential for slower growth

and the future return on the announced capital investment. We expect these concerns
to continue to linger well into next year as growth worries remain in France, bond
yields stay sticky around current levels, and, in the case of AI, investors demand more
compensation for financing.
At this stage, we do not expect the rising spreads in targeted areas to be a precursor
for a material slowdown in economic growth overall. Rather, we believe this is a natural

signal that investors are moving up the quality stack as we move into the middle of
the AI buildout. The bottom line is that rising credit spreads in lower quality corners of
the market are natural along the lifecycle of financing. When free cash flow is called
into question, or when debt financing costs begin to approach the expected return on
investment, investors begin to reprice risk.
We continue to emphasize higher quality balance sheets in corporate and sovereign

credit as well as higher quality leaders in the AI buildout overall. The closer we get to
2027, investors will likely begin to transition from too much exposure to the “buildout”
and not enough exposure to “AI adopters.”

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
SS&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and
services.
S&P 500 Market-cap Weighted Index is a float-adjusted market-capitalization-weighted index, meaning a company's influence and percentage weight in the index are determined by its
total market value (share price multiplied by the number of outstanding shares).

S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is a variation of the traditional S&P 500 where every single company in the index is given the exact same weight—0.2%—regardless of its size or market
capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Industrials Index imposes equal weights on the index constituents included in the S&P 500 that are classified in the GICS® industrials sector.
Russell 1000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of the 1,000 largest publicly traded companies in the United States.

This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.
The Chief Investment Office (“CIO”) provides thought leadership on wealth management, investment strategy and global markets; portfolio management solutions; due diligence; and solutions
oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

## Document 349: Global Equity Strategy_ 2027 Outlook_ Finding Value in Growth

### Page 1

2026 was the year of strong earnings. Global equity returns were unusually n
broad-based across regions, supported primarily by robust earnings growth — 
valuations have moderated. 
Earnings growth is poised to slow. As we enter 2027, earnings growth is on n
course to slow sharply across all major regions, growth in AI capex spending is 
likely to peak and the cost of capital remains elevated ... 
... But equities have de-rated sharply; with more attractive valuations, n
positive but slower nominal economic and earnings growth, and lower bond 
yields, inflation and oil prices, we expect equities to continue to rise, but 
with high volatility. 
We focus on Growth balanced with selective Defensives and beneficiaries of n
lower bond yields. 
Attractive opportunities are emerging in long-duration Growth companies, n
which should become more valuable as growth rates slow, particularly US 
Hyperscalers, which continue to enjoy strong (although slower) earnings growth 
and balance sheets. 
We balance this with selective defensive areas that offer reasonable valuations n
and areas that benefit from potential lower bond yields and oil prices. 
While forecast returns are highest in Asia, we focus on risk-adjusted returns and n
are overweight the US and Japan, neutral in Asia and EM, and underweight 
Europe, reflecting relative growth, profitability and valuation opportunities. 
Risks are becoming more two-sided. Bonds, oil prices and AI capex shifts could n
all increase volatility. We recommend diversification across Growth and 
selective Defensive strategies.

### Page 2

Value in Defensive Growth as earnings slow

Our theme for 2026 focused on a broadening equity bull market, with greater 
participation across regions, sectors and themes. Since then, the equity bull market has 
continued, and has widened out across the major regions despite rising oil prices and 
bond yields. The main driver has been strong corporate earnings growth across all 
regions. As we look to 2027, profit and AI capex growth look poised to slow in most 
regions. Nevertheless, recent price action has reflected this slowdown and equity 
valuations have come down substantially, particularly in the Technology sector. 
With economic activity and earnings on course to grow in 2027, albeit at a slower pace 
in nominal terms, and given the prospect of moderating inflation, bond yields and oil 
prices, we expect the equity bull market to continue. The range of potential outcomes 
has widened and, given stock concentration in the US and Asia, is particularly 
dependent on AI capex progress. Our recommended weightings reflect our 
assessment of risk-adjusted returns. We focus on: 
Growth — as earnings growth rates slow and margins moderate, investors are n
likely to reward these companies more highly, particularly as valuations become 
more attractive. AI-related companies have de-rated sharply, reflecting anxiety 
about peak AI capex spend and the possibility of slower returns. This is leaving 
attractive entry points, particularly in large capex Hyperscalers, which have strong 
balance sheets and diversified income streams. Significant upside potential has also 
opened up in more cyclical growth areas in memory and chips, although this comes 
with much higher risks and likely volatility.  
On a risk-adjusted basis, we are overweight the US and Japan, neutral Asia and n
EM, and underweight Europe.  
2026 — the year of earnings 
Key developments in 2026 form the foundations for a shift in opportunities in 2027. 
We see three standout features of global equity markets in 2026. 
Diversification has worked. Equity returns have been reasonable and very 1.
similar across all regions — reversing the trend between 2009 and 2025 when 
equity returns were consistently dominated by US outperformance. 
Strong earnings growth has been the principal driver of equity returns. 2.
All equity markets have de-rated, experiencing lower valuations. 3.
1. Diversified returns 
Overall, the past 12 months have seen broad-based returns across the regions. As 
Exhibit 1 shows, most equity markets have generated very similar total returns (the 
orange diamonds). This reverses the trend that had dominated post financial crisis 
through to 2025, when the US consistently outperformed other regions, benefiting from 
a combination of strong earnings growth and re-rating (as most of the businesses at the 
time were capital-light and high ROE). 
12 November 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Equity Strategy

### Page 3

2. Earnings have been the main driver of returns 
Earnings revisions have also been strong across the board (Exhibit 2). There are broadly 
three key drivers of this strength. First, Technology earnings have continued to grow 
robustly; second, higher oil prices have boosted earnings across the commodity 
sector; and third, the spill-over effects of the Technology capex boom have 
generally boosted earnings in HALO (heavy asset, low obsolescence) industries, 
such as Industrials. These factors have also contributed to an unusual pattern of 
earnings upgrades for 2026, 2027. So far, despite the recent equity sell-off, earnings 
revisions have also increased for 2028.

### Page 4

While this has been true in every region, the strongest upgrades have come in EM, largely 
reflecting huge upgrades in the Semiconductor space. This matters, because outsized 
gains in Tech Hardware stocks have pushed EM index concentration to record highs — 
Korea and Taiwan now account for about half of the MSCI EM, with Info Tech at >40% 
and the top 3 stocks (the ‘AI trio’ of TSMC, Samsung Electronics and SK Hynix) at ~30%. 
The recent sell-off in chip names, reflecting fears of a slower roll-out of data centres, has 
pushed valuations down sharply, creating good upside convexity — although the range 
of outcomes is wide and highly dependent on the pace of AI capex growth. See from our 
Equity analysts: At the center of tensions around the AI datacenter build: Searching for value 
following the sector-wide pullback. 
3. Equity markets have de-rated 
Equity valuations have fallen across the board. While the US equity market remains the 
most expensive, it has moderated from close to its 100th percentile over 20 years to a 
P/E of 18.5x.  Asian markets and EM are now trading at P/E ratios well below their 
historical averages. Overall, P/E ratios are around their average over the past 35 years, 
and a similar pattern is true of PEG ratios (adjusting valuations for expected growth) 
(Exhibit 3). Of course, much of the fall in valuations, particularly in the 
Tech-concentrated US and North Asia equity markets, has been the principal driver of 
this de-rating.

### Page 5

The key drivers for 2027 
Equity markets typically rise so long as nominal GDP is growing, valuations are not 
excessive, and interest rates are not rising sharply.  Nevertheless, the momentum of 
growth matters. The shift lower in equity markets in recent weeks — centred around AI 
concerns — has rapidly priced in the prospect of weaker growth rates, and the sharp 
sector and factor rotations within equities have pushed up the valuation of Defensive 
and Value parts of the market. From here, we expect equities to generate positive 
returns (Exhibit 4), albeit with higher volatility than in recent years. We expect investors 
to seek companies that can sustain growth as it becomes more scarce.

Theme 1: Earnings growth is on course to slow 
Our economists expect a slowing in nominal GDP growth rates in 2027 as the AI 
investment boom peaks. At the same time, nearly all markets are likely to see their 
earnings growth rates halve at least (Exhibit 5): in the US, slowing from 36% to 11%, 
Europe from just around 18% to 7%, Japan from 24% to 9% and Asia from close to

Why should earnings growth slow down? 
(a) Hyperscaler capex growth should slow, which should result in decelerating 
earnings growth (Exhibit 6) for much of the AI infrastructure complex and weaken the 
support for some of the ‘picks and shovels’ of the AI-related capex boom, including 
Industrials. Consensus estimates show a slowdown in IT earnings growth from close to 
90% in 2026 to 45% in 2027 and expect Communication Services to slow from growth of 
34% in 2026 to -5% in 2027. (b) Furthermore, depreciation charges should rise 
alongside other costs, such as interest charges. Together, headwinds to growth should 
boost the attractiveness of companies that can sustain higher growth and stable 
margins. This would be relatively good for the US Hyperscalers (whose valuations have 
fallen), but raises more risks for the chip names and Industrial Cyclicals, which have 
enjoyed the benefit of accelerating AI-related capex.

### Page 8

Theme 2: Cost of capital is higher 
Why is the cost of capital higher? 
First, higher inflation has pushed up policy rates. Our rates strategists expect to see a 1.
modest tightening cycle, but slow progress towards cutting interest rates in 2027. 
Second, we have argued that this has reflected the combined impact of a surge in 2.
private sector demand for capital and rising government borrowing. Our bond 
strategists do see some relief in longer-term rates, which should be more supportive 
for equities (Exhibit 8) but our credit strategists expect credit spreads to widen from 
current levels.

Investment Themes 
Broadly, we would focus on three areas within equities: Growth, selective Defensives and 
beneficiaries of lower oil prices and bond yields. The expressions that best fit these 
themes vary somewhat across regions and there is some overlap, particularly between 
defensive areas and bond beneficiaries. Exhibit 9 shows a high level summary. More 
details can be found in the specific regional outlook reports.

### Page 9

Growth 
1) Growth versus Value 
For most of the period after the financial crisis through to the end of Covid-19, equity 
markets were dominated by Growth companies. A combination of surging technology 
profitability and ultra-low interest rates favoured Growth. At the same time, 
disinflationary pressures coupled with low energy prices and poor Banks returns 
(particularly in Europe), became headwinds for Value. Since 2022, however, the 
patterns have changed. Growth has still done well, but rising inflation and interest rates, 
coupled with higher energy prices and a capex boom, have strengthened the 
performance of many Value sectors. In all regions Value has sharply outperformed 
Growth in recent months (Exhibit 10).

Exhibit 9: We focus on three areas: Growth, selective Defensives and beneficiaries of lower oil prices and bond yields

relative to its own history (it is now in the 20th percentile of its historical band over the 
past 20 years), and relative to other sectors. The IT sector is now one of the cheapest 
sectors on a P/E basis, and trades on an overall P/E similar to our Value factor. Of course, 
one needs to be careful in interpreting this because it partly reflects the very low P/E for 
chip and memory stocks, especially in Asia. As the exhibit shows, IT in EM (dominated by 
a few chip stocks) is the cheapest it has been in the past 20 years, and much cheaper 
than other sectors. But even the Hyperscalers are now cheaper than defensive areas of 
the market, such as Consumer Staples, as well as now having an overall P/E of 19.7x well 
below the previous 20-year range.

Within the Technology space, Hyperscalers look particularly attractive. Not only has the 
P/E fallen sharply, but it has now virtually closed the gap with the other 495 companies 
in the US (Exhibit 13).

Europe offers a region where lower oil prices would provide a particularly strong support

Investment focus: Selective Defensives  
The economic outlook remains resilient, but risks are skewed to the downside. Slower 
EPS growth, tighter financial conditions, elevated borrowing costs and the risk of an 
earlier turn in the AI cycle argue for greater portfolio resilience. Bond yields should 
decline modestly, but credit spreads could widen as growth slows. However, Cyclicals 
have already underperformed, while many traditional Defensives no longer look cheap

(Exhibit 16).

We favour selective Defensives rather than a broad defensive shift.  We focus on 
defensive sectors that would also benefit from lower bond yields. Utilities and Telecoms 
in Europe, Healthcare and companies consistently buying back stock in the US, and Asian 
power companies are examples. Quality should benefit from slower growth and tighter 
financial conditions, but valuation discipline remains important. High dispersion should 
create a better environment for stock-picking, with a focus on stable earnings, structural 
growth, strong balance sheets and shareholder returns. Areas of focus include: US—
Healthcare, Power and Buyback Aristocrats; Asia—Shareholder-return strategies, 
secure dividends, strong balance sheets and companies with high and stable 
margins. In Europe, we would include Telecoms and Utilities. 
Investment focus: Selective beneficiaries of lower oil prices and bond 
yields 
Some normalisation in the Middle East and a recovery in Persian Gulf supply should 
reduce geopolitical risk premia and bring down commodity prices, including European 
gas prices. Our commodities team forecast that Brent will fall from around $100/bbl at 
end-2026 to $78/bbl at end-2027, turning the energy-price impulse from a 0.4pp drag 
on global growth into a modest boost in 2027. Combined with fading US tariff effects 
and easing AI-related supply pressures, this should lower inflation and help central 
banks bring it back towards target. The Fed and ECB should move towards rate cuts in 
late 2027, supporting modestly lower bond yields. Our rates forecasts remain more 
dovish than market pricing.  
Investment implications. Lower inflation and bond yields should support long-duration 
Growth and rate-sensitive sectors. Falling energy costs should strengthen consumers’

### Page 15

real incomes across regions and support corporate margins outside the Energy sector. 
Europe should be the clearest beneficiary because of its greater exposure to 
imported energy and natural gas. We would focus on selective US consumer 
exposure, including Consumer Discretionary and long-duration Growth. In Asia, we 
would target selective long-duration and rate-sensitive beneficiaries. In Europe, we 
favour Telecoms, Utilities, UK Real Estate, the DAX and Retailers. 
Overweight the US  
While the US equity market outperformed consistently over the 15 years post the 
financial crisis, its relative performance has faded since 2025. Over the course of 2026, 
the US equity market has seen more of a valuation squeeze than other markets; its ROE 
has increased relative to the rest of the world, while its relative P/B has contracted 
(Exhibit 17). Overall, we see US equity valuations as attractive versus history and also 
compared with other markets when adjusted for growth and likely volatility. We are 
underweight Europe; while lower oil prices would provide an important boost for 
European economies, it would also have a negative impact on earnings growth. 
Valuations have not fallen as much as in other regions, and the risks are higher given 
government debt issues and the heavy political calendar.

The devaluation of emerging market equities has been particularly notable through 
2026 (Exhibit 18). Driven by the AI investment boom, outsized gains in Tech Hardware 
stocks have pushed EM index concentration to record highs — Korea and Taiwan now 
account for about half of the MSCI EM, with Info Tech at >40% and the top 3 stocks (the 
‘AI trio’ of TSMC, Samsung Electronics and SK Hynix) at ~30%. Although the recent 
sell-off leaves room for a potential strong recovery, the risks remain higher than for the 
Hyperscalers. For MXAPJ (incl. Korea/Taiwan), we forecast a 17% USD price return and 
20% total return, supported by 26%/13% EPS growth in 2027/28 and a 9.3x target P/E, 
but Ex-Korea/Taiwan, we forecast 12% USD price return and 15% total return, and

### Page 16

across MXAPJ markets, the median USD total return is 13%, with an average of 15%. 
Given YTD annualised volatility of 24.2% for MXAPJ we recommend a neutral overall 
position for Asia x Japan and EM on a volatility-adjusted basis.

What are the risks? 
We see four in particular: bond volatility, oil volatility, weaker AI capex, and momentum 
concentration — each of which could generate both downside as well as upside risks for 
investors.  
1. The bond market 
The rise in bond yields through 2026 has, so far, largely been offset in equity markets by 
strong earnings growth. That said, it has contributed to lower P/E ratios, and the 
underperformance of sectors that are most sensitive to interest rates, such as Real 
Estate and Defensives, while contributing to the relative strength (until recently) of 
Cyclicals.   
Any relief in bond markets, leading to lower yields, would hamper the relative 
performance of Cyclicals and Financials and favour some of the more defensive 
areas of the equity markets — another reason to have some exposure to selective 
defensives and also sectors that are highly negatively correlated with bond yields.  
Sovereign debt concerns could result in a further spike in bond yields, with the risks 
highest in Europe during an election-heavy year, while an alleviation of inflationary 
pressures might lead to a rally.  
2. Oil prices  
The combination of rising oil prices and bond yields has been central to the movement in 
equity markets through 2026. Our commodity analysts forecast oil at $88/bbl for Brent

Exhibit 18: EM devalued sharply through 2026 compared with DM

Goldman Sachs Global Equity Strategy

### Page 17

and $78/bbl for WTI in 2027, with risks in both directions. In general, lower energy
prices would be good for equity markets, bringing down inflation and interest rate
expectations. On balance, we think this would boost longer duration growth valuations
(from the relatively depressed levels currently), while also supporting defensive growth.
By contrast, such an outcome would reduce profit growth expectations for Energy
companies — a factor that would likely weigh more on Europe given that Energy is a

relatively important contributor to earnings growth.
3. AI capex spend
AI capex spending has been an important support for equity markets over the past three
years, driving earnings growth across the Technology ecosystem, and beyond into the
capex beneficiaries. Our economists expect the AI capex boom to slow. This could lead
to a de-rating in sectors that have become the beneficiaries of the capex boom,
including parts of the Industrials sector, which has re-rated, but also memory and chips

stocks. The latter have de-rated sharply, making the entry point more attractive, but
volatility is likely to remain high.
4. Narrow Breadth & Concentration
Related to this point, the US and Asia equity markets have a high degree of stock
concentration in their indices. Narrow breadth can create more stock volatility, with the
dominant stocks rising or the rest of the market falling. Either way, the momentum factor
risks high volatility and having some diversification across Growth and some Defensives

should help to improve risk-adjusted returns.  From a geographic perspective, the two
most concentrated regions are the US and Asia. That said, given recent market action,
we think the large cap technology names in the US are more defensive that those in Asia.
On a risk-adjusted basis, therefore, we think the momentum and concentration risks are
higher in Asia and imply lower risk-adjusted returns.

## Document 375: Metals sell-off runs ahead of physical markets

It has been a diﬃcult week for metals. Copper is down more
than 8%, aluminium almost 6% and gold around 9% as a
stronger dollar, higher rate expectations and weaker risk
appetite weigh on the complex
Ewa Manthey
Metals have come under sharp pressure this week, despite continued tightness
in physical markets
The speed of the sell-oﬀ looks more like a sharp repricing of investor
exposure than a broad weakening in physical markets. Copper has
taken the biggest hit as investors unwind bullish positions and

reassess AI-related demand. Yet the sharp correction in prices
contrasts with still-tight physical markets.
Investors cut bullish metals positions

Investor selling weighs on copper
Copper is on track for one of its steepest weekly declines in a
decade.
We think the speed of the sell-oﬀ partly reﬂects an unwind of bullish
investor positioning. Both LME and COMEX net length have fallen
sharply from recent highs, while positioning across the wider
metals complex has also become less bullish.

Copper has also become more closely correlated with equity
markets, including AI-related stocks. The AI link matters for investor
sentiment, but it does not make copper's demand story dependent
on data centres alone. Grid investment and electriﬁcation remain
important sources of demand. But as investors cut exposure to AI-
related assets, copper has become more vulnerable to the same
selling pressure.

Copper could remain under pressure if funds continue to cut
positions or the macro backdrop deteriorates. But the price
correction has moved much faster than physical conditions. Tight
availability in China should provide some support once the investor
selling eases.
The risk is that more US metal returns to the wider market if tariﬀ
expectations fade further, gradually easing some of the regional
tightness now supporting the physical market.
Aluminium supply remains tight for now

Aluminium has fallen with the wider complex, but nearby supply
remains tight. The LME cash-to-three-month spread has moved
into a steep backwardation, while LME stocks remain historically
low and largely of Russian origin, limiting what is readily available
to some buyers. This should help contain the near-term downside.
At the same time, investors have reduced bullish aluminium
positions from recent highs, in line with the broader move across
metals.

The medium-term picture is less supportive. Middle Eastern
production is recovering, and new Indonesian capacity is coming
online. We expect the market to move towards surplus next year,
which should limit the scope for a sustained price recovery.

Gold feels the pressure from higher rates
Gold has fallen for ﬁve straight sessions and is down around 9%

this week, putting it on course for its steepest weekly decline since
March. Prices have also touched their lowest level since September
2025.
The shift in the macro backdrop has weighed on gold, with higher
rate expectations and a stronger dollar reducing its appeal. The
equity sell-oﬀ may also have prompted some investors to sell gold
to raise cash.
We expect gold to remain vulnerable while rate expectations and
the dollar remain elevated. Central bank demand remains an

important longer-term support, with China adding 26 tonnes to its
reserves in October. But for now, macro forces are likely to set the
direction.
